  [Clipboard]
  Fix the mobile responsive layout issue in the Admin "Create Client" modal.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  - Do NOT change the existing functionality, API, validation, fields, or backend logic.
  - Do NOT remove any existing fields.
  - Do NOT change the desktop/laptop UI unnecessarily.
  - This is a responsive UI/layout fix only.
  
  CURRENT ISSUE:
  On desktop/laptop, the "Create Client" modal correctly shows:
  - Cancel button
  - Save Client button
  
  But on mobile screen, when opening the Create Client modal, the modal content becomes taller than the viewport and the bottom action section containing:
  - Cancel
  - Save Client
  
  is not visible / gets pushed below the screen.
  
  I have provided screenshots showing the difference between desktop and mobile.
  
  REQUIREMENTS:
  
  1. Inspect the existing Create Client modal component and its parent/container styles.
  
  2. Make the modal fully responsive for mobile screens.
  
  3. On mobile:
     - Modal must fit within the viewport.
     - Modal should have a maximum height such as:
       max-height: 90vh or an appropriate responsive value.
     - The form/content area should become scrollable.
     - The footer/action section must remain accessible.
     - "Cancel" and "Save Client" must always be reachable without breaking the modal.
  
  4. Preferred mobile structure:
  
     Modal
     ├── Header
     │   ├── Create Client
     │   └── Close
     │
     ├── Scrollable Form Content
     │   ├── Username
     │   ├── Full Name
     │   ├── Email
     │   ├── Mobile
     │   ├── Trading Status
     │   ├── License Type
     │   └── Expiry Date
     │
     └── Fixed/Sticky Footer
         ├── Cancel
         └── Save Client
  
  5. The footer should not disappear when the form is scrolled.
  
  6. On small mobile screens:
     - Use a single-column layout.
     - Reduce unnecessary horizontal padding/gaps.
     - Make inputs fit within the screen width.
     - Buttons must remain inside the viewport.
     - Prevent horizontal overflow.
  
  7. If the modal uses flexbox, use a structure similar to:
  
     display: flex;
     flex-direction: column;
     max-height: 90vh;
  
     Header:
     flex-shrink: 0;
  
     Form/content:
     flex: 1;
     overflow-y: auto;
     min-height: 0;
  
     Footer:
     flex-shrink: 0;
  
  Do NOT blindly copy this CSS. First inspect the current implementation and adapt it to the existing project architecture.
  
  8. On mobile, the footer can use:
     display: flex;
     gap: 12px;
  
     and make buttons appropriately sized.
  
  9. Ensure the footer has enough bottom spacing for mobile browser safe areas:
     padding-bottom: max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-bottom));
  
  10. If the modal is inside another container with:
     - overflow: hidden
     - fixed height
     - max-height
     - transform
     - position issues
  
     identify whether that parent is preventing the modal from scrolling or displaying the footer and fix the root cause.
  
  11. Check these screen sizes at minimum:
     - 320px width
     - 375px width
     - 390px width
     - 414px width
     - 768px width
     - Desktop
  
  12. Desktop behavior must remain the same as the current screenshot:
     - Two-column form layout where currently used.
     - Cancel button visible.
     - Save Client button visible.
     - Same overall design/theme.
  
  13. Mobile behavior should be:
     - One-column form.
     - Scrollable form.
     - Always accessible Cancel + Save Client buttons.
     - No content hidden behind the browser viewport.
     - No horizontal scrolling.
  
  14. Check whether the modal body currently contains all form fields but the footer is simply below the visible viewport. If yes, do not remove fields; make only the body scrollable and keep the footer visible.
  
  15. After making the fix:
     - Run the frontend build.
     - Check for TypeScript/ESLint errors.
     - Verify the Create Client modal on desktop and mobile.
     - Verify Cancel still closes the modal.
     - Verify Save Client still submits the form.
     - Verify validation errors remain visible and usable.
     - Verify scrolling works correctly when the keyboard opens on mobile.
  
  16. Do not introduce Firebase or any new dependency.
  
  17. Do not modify backend APIs.
  
  FINAL RESPONSE:
  Report:
  - Which component/file was changed
  - Root cause of the mobile issue
  - Exact responsive fix applied
  - Desktop verification
  - Mobile verification
  - Build result

  [LMB]
  [Clipboard]
  Implement a proper Admin Wallet Adjustment + Wallet Ledger system in my existing trading application.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT simply overwrite the user's wallet balance.
  
  For example, if the user's current wallet is ₹10,000 and admin wants to make it ₹20,000, the system must record the ₹10,000 difference as an ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT transaction.
  
  The wallet balance, transaction history, trade profit and trade loss must remain consistent.
  
  ==================================================
  1. FIRST INSPECT EXISTING CODE
  ==================================================
  
  Before changing anything, inspect the existing implementation for:
  
  - User model
  - Wallet model
  - Wallet balance
  - Wallet service
  - Wallet controller
  - Wallet routes
  - Transaction/Ledger model
  - Trade model
  - Trade execution service
  - Trade Profit/Loss calculation
  - Admin dashboard
  - User mobile wallet/account screen
  
  Understand how the existing system currently calculates wallet balance and P&L.
  
  DO NOT create a duplicate wallet system if one already exists.
  
  Reuse the existing wallet/ledger architecture wherever possible.
  
  ==================================================
  2. ADMIN WALLET MANAGEMENT
  ==================================================
  
  Add wallet management to the Admin Dashboard.
  
  Admin should be able to:
  
  - Search/select a user
  - See current wallet balance
  - Add funds
  - Deduct funds
  - Set a specific wallet balance
  - Enter a reason/note
  - Confirm the change
  
  Example:
  
  Current Wallet:
  ₹10,000
  
  Admin selects:
  
  Action:
  SET BALANCE
  
  New Balance:
  ₹20,000
  
  Reason:
  Manual wallet adjustment
  
  After confirmation:
  
  Wallet:
  ₹20,000
  
  ==================================================
  3. VERY IMPORTANT — LEDGER BASED BALANCE
  ==================================================
  
  NEVER do this:
  
  user.walletBalance = 20000
  
  without creating a corresponding transaction.
  
  Instead, calculate the difference.
  
  Example:
  
  Current Balance = ₹10,000
  Target Balance = ₹20,000
  
  Difference = +₹10,000
  
  Create:
  
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  Amount = +₹10,000
  
  Previous Balance = ₹10,000
  New Balance = ₹20,000
  
  Reason = "Manual wallet adjustment"
  
  Admin = currently authenticated admin
  
  Then update the user's wallet balance using the existing wallet architecture.
  
  ==================================================
  4. ADD FUNDS
  ==================================================
  
  If current wallet:
  
  ₹10,000
  
  Admin chooses:
  
  ADD FUNDS
  Amount = ₹5,000
  
  Result:
  
  Previous Balance = ₹10,000
  Adjustment = +₹5,000
  New Balance = ₹15,000
  
  Create a ledger transaction:
  
  type:
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  
  direction:
  CREDIT
  
  amount:
  5000
  
  previousBalance:
  10000
  
  newBalance:
  15000
  
  adminId:
  authenticated admin
  
  reason:
  provided admin reason
  
  ==================================================
  5. DEDUCT FUNDS
  ==================================================
  
  If current wallet:
  
  ₹15,000
  
  Admin chooses:
  
  DEDUCT FUNDS
  Amount = ₹3,000
  
  Result:
  
  Previous Balance = ₹15,000
  Adjustment = -₹3,000
  New Balance = ₹12,000
  
  Create a ledger transaction:
  
  type:
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  
  direction:
  DEBIT
  
  amount:
  3000
  
  previousBalance:
  15000
  
  newBalance:
  12000
  
  adminId:
  authenticated admin
  
  reason:
  provided admin reason
  
  ==================================================
  6. SET BALANCE
  ==================================================
  
  If:
  
  Current Balance = ₹10,000
  
  Admin chooses:
  
  SET BALANCE = ₹20,000
  
  DO NOT create a fake ₹20,000 deposit.
  
  Calculate:
  
  difference = 20,000 - 10,000
  difference = +10,000
  
  Create:
  
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  direction = CREDIT
  amount = 10,000
  previousBalance = 10,000
  newBalance = 20,000
  
  If:
  
  Current Balance = ₹20,000
  
  Admin sets:
  
  ₹15,000
  
  Then:
  
  difference = 15,000 - 20,000
  difference = -₹5,000
  
  Create:
  
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  direction = DEBIT
  amount = ₹5,000
  previousBalance = ₹20,000
  newBalance = ₹15,000
  
  This is critical for maintaining accurate accounting history.
  
  ==================================================
  7. WALLET LEDGER
  ==================================================
  
  Every balance-changing event must have a transaction record.
  
  At minimum distinguish:
  
  - ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  - TRADE_PROFIT
  - TRADE_LOSS
  - Existing deposit/payment transaction types
  - Existing withdrawal types
  - Any other existing wallet transaction types
  
  DO NOT convert trade profit/loss into ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT.
  
  Trade P&L must remain separate.
  
  Example:
  
  Wallet History:
  
  11 Aug 2026
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  +₹10,000
  Balance: ₹20,000
  Reason: Manual wallet adjustment
  
  11 Aug 2026
  TRADE_PROFIT
  +₹2,000
  Balance: ₹22,000
  
  11 Aug 2026
  TRADE_LOSS
  -₹1,000
  Balance: ₹21,000
  
  This way we can always understand where the balance came from.
  
  ==================================================
  8. TRADE PROFIT / LOSS
  ==================================================
  
  DO NOT break existing trade logic.
  
  Existing trading system should continue calculating:
  
  Entry Price
  Exit Price
  Quantity
  Profit
  Loss
  Net P&L
  
  For example:
  
  BUY
  Entry = ₹100
  Exit = ₹120
  Quantity = 100
  
  Profit:
  
  (120 - 100) × 100
  = ₹2,000
  
  This should be recorded as:
  
  TRADE_PROFIT
  +₹2,000
  
  For a loss:
  
  Entry = ₹120
  Exit = ₹100
  Quantity = 100
  
  Loss:
  
  (100 - 120) × 100
  = -₹2,000
  
  Record:
  
  TRADE_LOSS
  -₹2,000
  
  Use the existing project's P&L calculation if already implemented.
  Do NOT replace working trading logic unnecessarily.
  
  ==================================================
  9. FINAL WALLET BALANCE
  ==================================================
  
  Wallet balance must correctly account for all balance-affecting transactions.
  
  Conceptually:
  
  Opening Balance
  + Admin Credits
  - Admin Debits
  + Trade Profits
  - Trade Losses
  + Existing Deposits
  - Existing Withdrawals
  +/- Other existing wallet transactions
  
  Do not double-count anything.
  
  If the existing application already has a canonical wallet balance, use it as the authoritative balance and keep the ledger synchronized.
  
  ==================================================
  10. DATABASE TRANSACTION / ATOMICITY
  ==================================================
  
  Admin wallet modification must be atomic.
  
  Perform:
  
  BEGIN TRANSACTION
  
  1. Validate admin
  2. Validate user
  3. Lock/read current wallet balance
  4. Calculate adjustment
  5. Update wallet
  6. Create ledger transaction
  7. Commit
  
  If anything fails:
  
  ROLLBACK
  
  The wallet update and ledger record must never become inconsistent.
  
  Handle concurrent admin updates safely.
  
  For example:
  
  Admin A:
  ₹10,000 → ₹20,000
  
  Admin B simultaneously:
  ₹10,000 → ₹15,000
  
  The system must not corrupt the balance or ledger.
  
  Use the existing database ORM's transaction/locking mechanism.
  
  ==================================================
  11. SECURITY
  ==================================================
  
  Only authenticated admins can modify wallet balances.
  
  Normal users must NOT be able to call the wallet adjustment endpoint.
  
  Backend must validate:
  
  - Admin authorization
  - User ID
  - Amount
  - Adjustment type
  - Reason
  - Positive finite numbers
  - No NaN
  - No Infinity
  - No malformed values
  
  Never trust the balance sent by frontend.
  
  The backend must fetch the actual current balance from the database.
  
  ==================================================
  12. ADMIN UI
  ==================================================
  
  Add a Wallet Management section to the existing Admin Dashboard.
  
  Example:
  
  -----------------------------------
  Wallet Management
  -----------------------------------
  
  Select User
  [ Gaurav Patel ▼ ]
  
  Current Balance
  ₹10,000
  
  Action
  [ Set Balance ▼ ]
  
  Amount
  [ ₹20,000 ]
  
  Reason
  [ Manual wallet adjustment ]
  
  [ Cancel ] [ Update Wallet ]
  
  -----------------------------------
  
  After update:
  
  Current Balance
  ₹20,000
  
  Show success notification.
  
  Also show wallet transaction history.
  
  ==================================================
  13. WALLET HISTORY UI
  ==================================================
  
  Admin should be able to see:
  
  Date
  Transaction Type
  Credit/Debit
  Amount
  Previous Balance
  New Balance
  Reason
  Admin
  
  Example:
  
  | Date | Type | Direction | Amount | Previous | New |
  |------|------|-----------|--------|----------|-----|
  | 11 Aug | ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT | CREDIT | ₹10,000 | ₹10,000 | ₹20,000 |
  | 11 Aug | TRADE_PROFIT | CREDIT | ₹2,000 | ₹20,000 | ₹22,000 |
  | 11 Aug | TRADE_LOSS | DEBIT | ₹1,000 | ₹22,000 | ₹21,000 |
  
  ==================================================
  14. USER MOBILE APP
  ==================================================
  
  When admin changes the wallet:
  
  Admin:
  ₹10,000 → ₹20,000
  
  User mobile app must eventually show:
  
  ₹20,000
  
  Do not change existing mobile UI unnecessarily.
  
  Use the existing wallet API/state management.
  
  If the app already uses WebSocket updates, integrate with that.
  
  Otherwise refresh the wallet from the existing API when the wallet/account screen loads.
  
  DO NOT add Firebase.
  
  ==================================================
  15. P&L DISPLAY
  ==================================================
  
  Keep wallet balance and trading P&L logically separate.
  
  User should be able to see:
  
  Wallet Balance
  ₹20,000
  
  Total Profit
  ₹5,000
  
  Total Loss
  ₹2,000
  
  Net P&L
  +₹3,000
  
  Do not calculate Total Profit/Loss from admin wallet adjustments.
  
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT must NOT appear as trade profit or trade loss.
  
  ==================================================
  16. BACKEND API
  ==================================================
  
  First inspect existing wallet APIs.
  
  If existing APIs can support this, extend them.
  
  Otherwise create appropriate APIs such as:
  
  GET
  /admin/users/:userId/wallet
  
  POST
  /admin/users/:userId/wallet/adjust
  
  GET
  /admin/users/:userId/wallet/transactions
  
  Example request:
  
  {
    "type": "SET_BALANCE",
    "amount": 20000,
    "reason": "Manual wallet adjustment"
  }
  
  Backend should calculate the actual adjustment from the database balance.
  
  Example:
  
  DB balance = ₹10,000
  Requested target = ₹20,000
  
  Backend calculates:
  
  adjustment = +₹10,000
  
  Never trust:
  
  previousBalance
  
  from frontend.
  
  ==================================================
  17. IMPORTANT ACCOUNTING RULE
  ==================================================
  
  Do NOT make the wallet balance change silently.
  
  Every manual admin change must be traceable.
  
  If admin changes:
  
  ₹10,000 → ₹20,000
  
  the system must show:
  
  ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT
  +₹10,000
  
  Reason:
  Manual wallet adjustment
  
  Admin:
  <admin name>
  
  Timestamp:
  <timestamp>
  
  This is required so wallet history never becomes confusing later.
  
  ==================================================
  18. DO NOT BREAK EXISTING FEATURES
  ==================================================
  
  Do NOT change or break:
  
  - User authentication
  - Admin authentication
  - Admin trade assignment
  - Trade execution
  - AngelOne integration
  - TickEngine
  - Redis
  - Market data
  - Option chain
  - Instrument sync
  - User view-only restrictions
  - Existing wallet payment flow
  - Existing mobile app functionality
  
  Only integrate the new wallet management functionality with the existing architecture.
  
  ==================================================
  19. TEST THESE EXACT CASES
  ==================================================
  
  CASE 1:
  
  Initial:
  ₹10,000
  
  Admin:
  SET BALANCE ₹20,000
  
  Expected:
  Wallet = ₹20,000
  Ledger = ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT +₹10,000
  
  CASE 2:
  
  Current:
  ₹20,000
  
  Admin:
  SET BALANCE ₹15,000
  
  Expected:
  Wallet = ₹15,000
  Ledger = ADMIN_ADJUSTMENT -₹5,000
  
  CASE 3:
  
  Current:
  ₹15,000
  
  ADD ₹5,000
  
  Expected:
  ₹20,000
  Ledger +₹5,000
  
  CASE 4:
  
  Current:
  ₹20,000
  
  DEDUCT ₹3,000
  
  Expected:
  ₹17,000
  Ledger -₹3,000
  
  CASE 5:
  
  Wallet:
  ₹20,000
  
  Trade Profit:
  ₹2,000
  
  Expected:
  Wallet follows existing trading accounting rules
  Trade P&L = +₹2,000
  Ledger type = TRADE_PROFIT
  
  CASE 6:
  
  Trade Loss:
  ₹1,000
  
  Expected:
  Trade P&L = -₹1,000
  Ledger type = TRADE_LOSS
  
  CASE 7:
  
  Admin adjustment + trade profit + trade loss.
  
  Verify all three remain separate and correctly represented.
  
  CASE 8:
  
  Normal user tries admin wallet API.
  
  Expected:
  401/403
  
  CASE 9:
  
  Invalid amount.
  
  Expected:
  Validation error.
  
  CASE 10:
  
  Database error during wallet update.
  
  Expected:
  Wallet update and ledger transaction both rollback.
  
  ==================================================
  20. BUILD AND FINAL VERIFICATION
  ==================================================
  
  After implementation:
  
  Backend:
  npm run build
  
  Web frontend:
  npm run build
  
  Mobile:
  Run the existing mobile project's appropriate typecheck/build command.
  
  Verify:
  
  - Admin can adjust wallet
  - User cannot adjust wallet
  - Balance updates correctly
  - Ledger entry created
  - Previous balance correct
  - New balance correct
  - Trade profit remains separate
  - Trade loss remains separate
  - Wallet history is correct
  - Mobile wallet reflects new balance
  - Existing trading functionality remains unchanged
  - No Firebase added
  - No unnecessary dependencies added
  
  ==================================================
  FINAL REPORT
  ==================================================
  
  Provide a report containing:
  
  1. Existing wallet architecture discovered
  2. Files changed
  3. Database/schema changes
  4. APIs added/modified
  5. Admin UI changes
  6. Mobile UI changes
  7. Ledger implementation
  8. Admin adjustment calculation
  9. Trade P&L integration
  10. Authorization/security
  11. Atomic transaction handling
  12. Test results
  13. Build results
  14. Any remaining issues
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do not claim production/runtime testing unless actually performed.
  Clearly separate code verification, build verification, local testing, and production testing.
  [Tab]

  export const getClients = async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response) => {
    try {
      const variant = String(req.query.variant || 'all').toLowerCase();
      const { page = 1, limit = 20, search } = req.query;
      const query = pickVariantQuery(variant);
  
      if (search) {
        const searchOr = [
          { user_name: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
          { email: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
          { full_name: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
        ];
        if (query.$or) {
          const variantOr = query.$or;
          delete query.$or;
          query.$and = [{ $or: variantOr }, { $or: searchOr }];
        } else {
          query.$or = searchOr;
        }
      }
  
      const skip = (Number(page) - 1) * Number(limit);
      const sort: Record<string, 1 | -1> =
        variant === 'signup'
          ? { created_at: -1 }
          : variant === 'star'
          ? { updated_at: -1 }
          : { created_at: -1 };
  
      const users = await User.find(query)
        .select('-password')
        .skip(skip)
        .limit(Number(limit))
        .sort(sort);
  
      const total = await User.countDocuments(query);
  
      return res.json({
        success: true,
        data: {
          users,
          total,
          page: Number(page),
          pages: Math.ceil(total / Number(limit)),
          variant,
        },
      });
    } catch (error: any) {
      return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: error.message });
    }
  };
  
  export const toggleStarClient = async (req: AuthRequest, res: Response) => {
    try {
      const user = await User.findById(req.params.id);
      if (!user) {
        return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: 'User not found' });
      }
      user.is_star = !Boolean(user.is_star);
      await user.save();
      return res.json({ success: true, data: user });
    } catch (error: any) {
      return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: error.message });
    }
  };
  
  export const getRiskHealth = async (_req: AuthRequest, res: Response) => {
    try {
      const [activeUsers, suspendedUsers, activeTrades, pendingDeposits, pendingWithdrawals, openTickets] = await Promise.all([
        User.countDocuments({ role: 'user', status: 'active' }),
        User.countDocuments({ role: 'user', status: 'suspended' }),
        Trade.countDocuments({ status: 'ACTIVE' }),
        Deposit.countDocuments({ status: 'PENDING' }),
        Withdrawal.countDocuments({ status: 'PENDING' }),
        Ticket.countDocuments({ status: 'Open' }),
      ]);
  
      const riskScore = Math.min(100, suspendedUsers * 10 + pendingDeposits * 5 + pendingWithdrawals * 5 + openTickets * 3 + activeTrades);
  
      return res.json({
        success: true,
        data: {
          riskScore,
          riskLevel: riskScore > 60 ? 'Elevated' : riskScore > 30 ? 'Moderate' : 'Stable',
          activeUsers,
          suspendedUsers,
          activeTrades,
          pendingDeposits,
          pendingWithdrawals,
          openTickets,
          checks: [
            { label: 'Wallet system', ok: true },
            { label: 'KYC flow', ok: true },
  root@srv1158034:/opt/alpha-research/backend# mongosh "mongodb://127.0.0.1:27017/prime-alpha-research" --eval '

  I need you to investigate and FIX a production issue in my Alpha Research project.
  
  PROBLEM:
  Clients created/available in the local environment are visible in the Admin → Clients page, but the clients that exist in the production/live environment are not appearing in the Admin → Clients page.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT assume that MongoDB is the problem.
  Do NOT manually insert/update/delete production database records.
  Do NOT change unrelated trading, wallet, authentication, order, or market-data logic.
  
  CURRENT PRODUCTION EVIDENCE:
  
  Production MongoDB:
  - Database: prime-alpha-research
  - users collection: 4 documents
  - wallets collection: 4 documents
  - trades collection: 2 documents
  - clientsaves collection: 0 documents
  
  Backend MongoDB connection is working:
  "Successfully connected to MongoDB"
  
  Frontend calls:
  GET /admin/clients?variant=all&limit=200
  
  Backend route:
  src/routes/admin.routes.ts
  
  Route:
  router.get('/clients', protect, adminOnly, getClients);
  
  Controller:
  src/controllers/admin.controller.ts
  
  getClients() currently does:
  
  const variant = String(req.query.variant || 'all').toLowerCase();
  const { page = 1, limit = 20, search } = req.query;
  const query = pickVariantQuery(variant);
  
  if (search) {
    const searchOr = [
      { user_name: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
      { email: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
      { full_name: { $regex: search, $options: 'i' } },
    ];
  
    if (query.$or) {
      const variantOr = query.$or;
      delete query.$or;
      query.$and = [{ $or: variantOr }, { $or: searchOr }];
    } else {
      query.$or = searchOr;
    }
  }
  
  const skip = (Number(page) - 1) * Number(limit);
  
  const users = await User.find(query)
    .select('-password')
    .skip(skip)
    .limit(Number(limit))
    .sort(sort);
  
  const total = await User.countDocuments(query);
  
  return res.json({
    success: true,
    data: {
      users,
      total,
      page: Number(page),
      pages: Math.ceil(total / Number(limit)),
      variant,
    },
  });
  
  IMPORTANT FRONTEND FILES:
  - src/screens/admin/Clients.tsx
  - src/screens/admin/PendingClients.tsx
  - src/screens/admin/Dashboard.tsx
  - src/components/admin/AdminLayout.tsx
  
  Frontend references:
  - /admin/clients?variant=all&limit=200
  - /admin/clients?variant=pending&limit=200
  - /admin/clients?variant=all&limit=5
  
  BACKEND ROUTES ALSO FOUND:

  - src/routes/admin_modules.routes.ts
  - src/routes/broker-user.routes.ts
  - src/routes/appAuth.routes.ts
  
  There are multiple client-related APIs, so do NOT assume all client screens use the same collection/API.
  
  PRIMARY INVESTIGATION:
  
  1. Find and inspect:
     src/controllers/admin.controller.ts
  
  2. Specifically inspect:
     pickVariantQuery()
  
  3. Trace exactly what query is generated for:
     variant=all
  
  4. Inspect the User model/schema and determine:
     - role field
     - status field
     - is_active field
     - is_star field
     - deleted/deactivated fields
     - created_at
     - any admin/owner relationship
     - any field used by pickVariantQuery()
  
  5. Compare the expected filter with the actual production users.
  
  6. Inspect:
     src/screens/admin/Clients.tsx
  
     Verify that the frontend correctly reads:
     response.data.data.users
  
     and is not expecting:
     response.data.data.clients
  
  7. Inspect the API client/base URL configuration and make sure production Admin → Clients is calling the correct backend endpoint.
  
  8. Check for local vs production API contract/version mismatch.
  
  9. Search the entire repository for all usages of:
     - /admin/clients
     - getClients
     - pickVariantQuery
     - User.find(
     - role: 'user'
     - status: 'active'
     - is_active
     - deleted
     - client
  
  10. Determine whether clients are supposed to be stored in:
     - users
     - clientsaves
     - or another collection
  
  Based on the current getClients() code, /admin/clients appears to use the users collection. Do not change this assumption unless the codebase proves otherwise.
  
  PRODUCTION DATA SAFETY:
  
  Do NOT:
  - delete production users
  - modify production users
  - insert fake clients
  - migrate collections blindly
  - change passwords
  - modify wallet balances
  - modify trades
  - change broker credentials
  
  If a production DB check is needed, use READ-ONLY queries only.

  Frontend request

  - is_star
  - user_type
  - deleted flags
  - required client fields

  If the problem is caused by production schema differences, make the application logic robust rather than manually modifying production data.

  IMPORTANT:
  - First inspect the complete existing backend, web frontend, and mobile app architecture.
  - Reuse the existing authentication, wallet, trade, user, KYC, withdrawal, notification, broker, and admin systems wherever possible.

  - Do NOT remove or change existing trading functionality unless explicitly required below.
  - Preserve the current UI theme/design and make the new UI responsive for mobile and desktop.
  - Do NOT add Firebase. The project is NOT using Firebase and I do not want Firebase added.
  - Before changing code, identify the existing implementation and explain which files/modules will be changed.
  - After implementation, run/build/test backend, web frontend, and mobile app where applicable.

  1. USER MOBILE APP — NOTIFICATION ICON
  ==================================================
  
  In the user mobile dashboard shown in the attached screenshot, the notification icon/count is not displaying correctly.
  
  Fix the notification system so that:
  
  - Notification/bell icon is clearly visible on the dashboard/header.
  - If unread notifications exist, show an unread badge/count.
  - Clicking the notification icon opens the notification screen/list.
  - Show:
    - unread notifications
    - read notifications
    - notification title
    - message
    - date/time
  - Provide "Mark as read".
  - Provide "Mark all as read".
  - Unread count must update correctly.
  - If there are no notifications, show a clean empty state.
  - Notification count should update after reading notifications.
  - Use the existing notification backend/API if available.
  - Do not introduce Firebase.
  - Verify the mobile API authentication and user-specific notification filtering.
  
  Do not hard-code notification counts.
  
  ==================================================
  2. USER WALLET — SHOW TOTAL AMOUNT
  ==================================================
  
  The current dashboard shows:
  
  Portfolio Value (Equity)
  ₹5,895.21
  
  But I also need the user's actual wallet balance/total wallet amount to be clearly visible.
  
  Implement a proper Wallet section/card.
  
  Show at minimum:
  
  - Total Wallet Balance
  - Available Balance
  - Amount Used/Blocked
  - Total Deposited
  - Total Withdrawn
  - Realized Profit
  - Realized Loss
  - Current P&L
  - Brokerage/Charges
  - Net Balance
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do not calculate wallet balance only on the frontend.
  
  Wallet amount must come from the backend/database and use the existing wallet/transaction system.
  
  The balance must remain consistent after:
  - deposit
  - withdrawal
  - trade entry
  - trade exit
  - profit
  - loss
  - brokerage
  - other applicable charges
  
  Do not duplicate money in multiple places.
  
  ==================================================
  3. USER WITHDRAWAL — ADD BANK ACCOUNT
  ==================================================
  
  Users must be able to add their own bank account from the mobile app before requesting withdrawal.
  
  Create/verify a secure Bank Account section.
  
  User should be able to add:
  
  - Account Holder Name
  - Bank Name
  - Account Number
  - Confirm Account Number
  - IFSC Code
  - Account Type
  - Optional UPI ID if existing architecture supports it
  
  Security requirements:
  
  - Never expose full bank account number unnecessarily.
  - Mask account number in UI, e.g. **** **** 1234.
  - Do not log sensitive bank details.
  - Validate IFSC format.
  - Confirm account number before saving.
  - Prevent invalid/duplicate bank accounts where appropriate.
  - User can select a default bank account.
  - User can add/update/remove bank account according to existing business rules.
  
  Withdrawal flow:
  
  Wallet
  → Withdraw
  → Select/Add Bank Account
  → Enter amount
  → Show available balance
  → Show applicable charges
  → Confirm withdrawal
  → Submit withdrawal request
  
  Do not allow withdrawal above available balance.
  
  Do not directly deduct money without maintaining the existing transaction/withdrawal ledger.
  
  ==================================================

  ==================================================
  
  Admin currently needs a complete view of each client/user.
  
  When admin opens a client, create/improve a detailed Client Profile / Client Details page.
  
  Admin should be able to see all relevant existing user information, including:
  
  PROFILE:
  - Username
  - Full Name
  - Email
  - Mobile
  - User ID
  - Client Code
  - Account status
  - Registration date

  - License expiry
  
  KYC:
  - PAN details/status
  - Aadhaar details/status
  - KYC status
  - KYC verification status
  - Any other KYC information already stored by the application
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT invent KYC fields if they don't exist.
  First inspect existing User/KYC models and display whatever is actually available.
  
  BANK:
  - Bank account details saved by user
  - Bank name
  - Account holder name
  - Masked account number
  - IFSC
  - Account type
  - Default account
  - Bank verification/status
  
  BROKER:
  - Broker connected/not connected
  - Broker name
  - Broker client ID/code where already supported
  - Connection status
  - Existing broker-related status information
  
  WALLET:

  - Total deposits
  - Total withdrawals

  - Total loss

  ==================================================
  5. ADMIN WALLET VISIBILITY

  ==================================================
  6. BROKERAGE MUST BE ₹10
  ==================================================
  
  The brokerage/charge for a trade must be ₹10.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT implement this only in the frontend.
  
  Find the existing brokerage calculation/service used by the backend.
  
  Change the actual backend calculation so the applicable brokerage amount is ₹10 according to the application's existing trade/business model.
  
  Make sure the same brokerage value is reflected consistently in:
  
  - Trade execution
  - Trade details
  - Wallet
  - P&L
  - Transaction records
  - Admin client details
  - User trade history
  - Any applicable reports
  
  Do not accidentally charge ₹10 multiple times for the same trade.
  
  Before modifying this, inspect how brokerage is currently calculated and determine whether the existing system charges:
  - per order
  - per completed trade
  - per side
  - per quantity
  
  Preserve the existing business model and apply ₹10 correctly.
  
  Do not create fake frontend calculations.
  
  ==================================================
  7. PROFIT / LOSS MUST UPDATE ACCOUNT
  ==================================================
  
  Existing trading functionality must remain unchanged.
  
  When a trade closes:
  
  - Calculate actual P&L using the existing backend trade logic.
  - Apply ₹10 brokerage according to the rule above.
  - Update the user's wallet/account balance through the existing wallet/transaction system.
  - Store the transaction/ledger entry.
  - Show updated amount to the user.
  - Show updated P&L to admin.
  - Keep trade history consistent.
  
  Example:
  
  Trade profit = ₹500
  Brokerage = ₹10
  Net profit = ₹490
  
  If loss:
  
  Trade loss = ₹500
  Brokerage = ₹10
  Net loss = ₹510
  
  Use the existing application's financial calculation conventions if they differ in a legitimate way; do not duplicate balance updates.
  
  ==================================================
  8. ADMIN SECURITY
  ==================================================
  
  Admin must be able to see sensitive client details only if the existing admin permission system allows it.
  
  Backend must enforce:
  
  - admin authentication
  - admin authorization
  - existing permissions/roles
  - user ownership rules where applicable
  
  Never rely only on frontend route protection.
  
  Do not expose:
  - passwords
  - auth tokens
  - broker secrets
  - API keys
  - TOTP secrets
  - sensitive credentials
  
  PAN/Aadhaar/bank information should be handled securely and masked where appropriate.

  pr mujhe ye thoda chota chahiye mtln [Bk][Bk]b rk [Bk][Bk][Bk]ek pr[Bk]rofectional  dikh [ENTER]

  [Clipboard]
  I need you to improve ONLY the UI/UX and layout of the existing Admin Trade Details page.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  - Do NOT change any existing business logic.
  - Do NOT change API endpoints.
  - Do NOT change database models.
  - Do NOT change trade calculations, P&L calculations, entry/exit logic, or status logic.
  - Do NOT remove any existing functionality.
  - Keep all current data and functionality exactly as it is.
  - First inspect the existing implementation and reuse the current components, API calls, types, and styling system.
  
  CURRENT ISSUE:
  The Trade Details page is working correctly, but the current layout is too large and takes too much vertical/horizontal space. It looks oversized and does not feel like a professional trading/admin dashboard.
  
  GOAL:
  Make the Trade Details page more compact, clean, professional, modern, and easy to scan.
  
  DESIGN REQUIREMENTS:
  
  1. PAGE HEADER
  - Keep the existing page title and navigation.
  - Reduce unnecessary vertical spacing.
  - Make the header compact.
  - Keep Refresh and other existing actions working.
  - Maintain the current overall admin dashboard theme.
  
  2. TRADE TABLE
  Make the trade table significantly more compact.
  
  Current columns include:
  - Client
  - Symbol
  - Strategy
  - Entry/Exit
  - P&L
  - Status
  - Existing action/status controls
  
  Keep ALL existing columns and functionality.
  
  Improve:
  - Reduce row height.
  - Reduce excessive padding.
  - Reduce font sizes slightly while keeping readability.
  - Client name should be prominent but compact.
  - Client ID can be smaller/muted.
  - Symbol should be visually clear.
  - BUY/SELL should have a compact badge.
  - Strategy should use a small badge/text.
  - Entry and Exit should be displayed together in a compact block:
    Entry: ₹100.00
    Exit: ₹250.00
  - P&L should be visually prominent but compact.
  - Status should use a small professional badge.
  - Existing action controls must remain functional.
  
  3. TABLE ROW DESIGN
  Instead of huge rows, create a professional compact trading-terminal style row.
  
  Target:
  - Approximately 65–85px row height depending on content.
  - Avoid unnecessary empty vertical space.
  - Use subtle borders/dividers.
  - Keep enough spacing so the table does not feel crowded.
  
  4. TRADE SUMMARY SIDEBAR
  The Trade Summary section on the right is currently too large.
  
  Make it smaller and more compact.
  
  Keep:
  - Active Trade Count
  - Closed Trade Count
  - Existing informational text
  
  Improve:
  - Reduce card height.
  - Reduce padding.
  - Reduce font sizes.
  - Use compact statistic cards.
  - Keep numbers clearly visible.
  - Make the sidebar width smaller where possible.
  
  Suggested layout:
  
  Trade Summary
  -------------------------
  Active Trades      0
  Closed Trades      3
  
  [small informational card]
  
  Do not remove any existing summary information.
  
  5. DESKTOP LAYOUT
  Use the available screen space efficiently.
  
  Suggested proportions:
  - Main trade table: approximately 72–78%
  - Trade Summary: approximately 22–28%
  
  The table should have enough space for all columns without looking oversized.
  
  Avoid unnecessary horizontal scrolling if it can be solved safely through better column sizing.
  
  6. RESPONSIVE DESIGN
  This is very important.
  
  Desktop:
  - Compact two-column layout.
  - Table + summary sidebar.
  
  Tablet:
  - Reduce spacing and sidebar width.
  
  Mobile:
  - Do NOT allow the entire page to become unusable because of the wide table.
  - Make the table horizontally scrollable only when necessary.
  - Keep important information visible.
  - Do not break existing actions/buttons.
  - Avoid oversized cards.
  
  7. VISUAL STYLE
  Keep the existing Prime Alpha admin dashboard style.
  
  Use:
  - Clean white/light background
  - Subtle borders
  - Rounded corners
  - Professional typography
  - Small status badges
  - Green for positive P&L
  - Red for negative P&L
  - Muted gray for secondary information
  - Existing brand colors where already defined
  
  Do NOT introduce a completely different design system.
  
  8. P&L DISPLAY
  Make P&L easier to scan.
  
  Example:
  
  +₹30,000.00
  green
  
  -₹5,000.00
  red
  
  Keep the existing actual P&L values and calculations.
  
  9. ENTRY / EXIT
  Make Entry/Exit more compact.
  
  Example:
  
  Entry ₹100.00
  Exit  ₹250.00
  
  Do not change the underlying values.
  
  10. CLIENT INFORMATION
  Keep:
  CLIENT NAME
  CLIENT ID
  
  But make the ID smaller and less visually dominant.
  
  Example:
  
  KHUSHBU GUPTA
  6a7af8b574e50a55d7100cf6
  
  11. DO NOT CHANGE FUNCTIONALITY
  Before making changes, inspect:
  - TradeDetails page
  - Related components
  - API calls
  - Type definitions
  - Existing CSS/Tailwind classes
  - Trade/P&L calculation logic
  
  Only modify the presentation/layout layer unless a tiny UI-only adjustment is required.
  
  12. CODE QUALITY
  - Reuse existing components.
  - Avoid duplicate code.
  - Keep TypeScript type safety.
  - Do not add unnecessary dependencies.
  - Do not introduce mock data.
  - Do not hardcode trade values.
  - Preserve loading states.
  - Preserve error states.
  - Preserve empty states.
  - Preserve pagination/filtering if currently implemented.
  - Preserve all API behavior.
  
  13. FINAL VERIFICATION
  After implementation:
  
  1. Run frontend lint/type checks if available.
  2. Run production build.
  3. Verify Trade Details page loads correctly.
  4. Verify active trades.
  5. Verify closed trades.
  6. Verify positive P&L.
  7. Verify negative P&L.
  8. Verify Entry/Exit values.
  9. Verify client names/IDs.
  10. Verify status/actions.
  11. Verify Trade Summary.
  12. Verify desktop/tablet/mobile responsiveness.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do not just make the page "smaller".
  Make it look like a professional production-grade trading admin terminal with better information density, spacing, hierarchy, and alignment.
  
  Before coding, inspect the existing Trade Details implementation and identify exactly which components/styles control the oversized layout. Then implement the UI improvements without changing existing functionality.
  [LMB]

  I need you to fix and polish the mobile app Notifications and Profile screens.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  - First inspect the existing mobile app code and understand the current navigation, bottom tab navigation, notification screen, profile screen, notification API/state management, and shared layout/components.
  - Do NOT rewrite unrelated functionality.
  - Do NOT change existing trading, wallet, portfolio, market, authentication, or other business logic.
  - Keep all existing functionality exactly as it is unless required for these fixes.
  - Do NOT add Firebase. The project is NOT using Firebase and must remain that way.
  - Reuse the existing design system, components, APIs, and data wherever possible.
  
  I have provided 3 screenshots as visual references.
  
  ==================================================
  1. NOTIFICATION SCREEN — FIX LAYOUT & DESIGN
  ==================================================
  
  The Notifications screen currently looks inconsistent and some elements overlap/misalign.
  
  Make the Notifications screen look like a real production mobile application.
  
  Requirements:
  
  - Keep a clean white/light background.
  - Add a proper top header:
    - Title: "Notifications"
    - Proper spacing from status bar
    - No duplicate "Notifications" title.
  - Remove any duplicated title/header currently appearing.
  - Subtitle:
    "Stay updated with market & account alerts"
  - Notification cards should be properly aligned and evenly spaced.
  - Each notification should be displayed as a clean card with:
    - Notification title
    - Description/message
    - Date/time aligned properly
  - Make sure long notification text wraps correctly.
  - No text should overflow outside the card.
  - Date/time must remain inside the card and align consistently.
  - Maintain proper horizontal padding on both sides.
  - Cards should have consistent:
    - border radius
    - border
    - padding
    - margin
    - typography
  - The notification list should be vertically scrollable.
  - Bottom navigation must NOT overlap notification cards.
  - Add enough bottom content padding so the last notification can be completely viewed above the bottom navigation.
  - Respect Android/iOS safe areas and status bar.
  
  Example notification types already present:
  - Trade Closed
  - Profit Credited
  - New Trade Assigned
  
  Do not replace real backend notification data with static/mock data.
  
  ==================================================
  2. MARK ALL AS READ
  ==================================================
  
  The current "Mark all as read" functionality shows a native-looking alert/dialog such as:
  
  "Success
  All notifications marked as read.
  OK"
  
  Make this behavior consistent with the app's existing UI.
  
  Requirements:
  - Do not break the existing API/functionality.
  - After successfully marking notifications as read, update the notification state immediately.
  - Unread badge/count should update immediately.
  - Avoid unnecessary reloads.
  - If the project already has a custom modal/toast component, use it instead of a default browser/native alert.
  - Keep the UI professional and consistent with the rest of the app.
  
  ==================================================
  3. PROFILE TAB IS SHOWING NOTIFICATIONS — CRITICAL BUG
  ==================================================
  
  This is the most important issue.
  
  When I click the "Profile" icon in the bottom navigation, the app is currently showing the Notifications screen instead of the Profile screen.
  
  Fix the navigation/state/routing issue.
  
  Expected behavior:
  
  BOTTOM NAVIGATION:
  
  Home      Portfolio      Market      Wallet      Profile
  
  When the user taps:
  
  Profile → MUST open the Profile screen.
  
  It must NOT open Notifications.
  
  Notifications should only open when:
  - the notification icon/header is tapped, OR
  - the existing Notifications navigation route is explicitly selected.
  
  Investigate:
  - Bottom tab route configuration
  - Navigation stack
  - Screen names
  - Route names
  - activeTab state
  - conditional rendering
  - navigation.navigate()
  - navigation.replace()
  - notification/profile route mapping
  - any shared screen/component state
  - any incorrect route alias
  - any accidental reuse of notification screen in Profile tab
  
  Do not solve this with a hardcoded visual workaround.
  
  Fix the actual root cause in navigation.
  
  ==================================================
  4. PROFILE SCREEN MUST REMAIN THE PROFILE SCREEN
  ==================================================
  
  After fixing navigation, verify the existing Profile screen.
  
  When Profile is selected, it should show the user's actual profile page and existing profile functionality.

  ==================================================

  ==================================================
  
  The notification icon should behave like a real notification system.

  - If there are unread notifications, show the unread badge/count.
  - Badge should update when:

  - Make sure the notification icon does not interfere with the Profile tab.
  - Notification navigation must be independent from bottom-tab navigation.
  
  If the backend already provides unread notification count, use that instead of creating duplicate counting logic.
  
  ==================================================

  ==================================================

  [LMB]
  [Clipboard]
  I need you to fix and improve the Wallet Withdrawal flow in my trading mobile app.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  - First inspect the complete existing wallet, withdrawal, transaction, notification, admin approval, and backend logic.
  - Do NOT rewrite unrelated functionality.
  - Keep all existing trading, portfolio, market, profile, authentication, broker, and other functionality unchanged.
  - Do NOT add Firebase.
  - Reuse the existing backend APIs, database models, wallet logic, notification system, and UI components wherever possible.
  - Do not use fake/mock wallet balances or withdrawal data.
  - This must be a real end-to-end production-ready implementation.
  
  I have provided screenshots showing the current Wallet UI and withdrawal behavior.
  
  ==================================================
  1. CRITICAL BUG — WITHDRAWAL IS DEDUCTING MONEY TOO EARLY
  ==================================================
  
  CURRENT PROBLEM:
  
  When a user submits a withdrawal request, the amount is immediately deducted from the user's wallet.
  
  Example:
  
  User wallet:
  ₹24,923.49
  
  User requests withdrawal:
  ₹5,000
  
  Currently the wallet immediately becomes approximately:
  ₹19,923.49
  
  This is WRONG.
  
  The amount must NOT be permanently deducted when the user simply submits a withdrawal request.
  
  ==================================================
  2. REQUIRED WITHDRAWAL LIFECYCLE
  ==================================================
  
  Implement this exact lifecycle:
  
  USER
    ↓
  Creates withdrawal request
    ↓
  Status = PENDING
    ↓
  Admin receives withdrawal request
    ↓
  Admin reviews request
    ↓
  Admin APPROVES
    ↓
  Money is deducted from wallet
    ↓
  Withdrawal is processed
    ↓
  User receives success notification
  
  OR
  
  USER
    ↓
  Creates withdrawal request
    ↓
  Status = PENDING
    ↓
  Admin REJECTS
    ↓
  No wallet deduction
    ↓
  Request status = REJECTED
    ↓
  User receives rejection notification
  
  ==================================================
  3. WALLET BALANCE RULE
  ==================================================
  
  The wallet balance must only be reduced after ADMIN APPROVAL.
  
  Example:
  
  Initial wallet:
  ₹24,923.49
  
  User requests:
  ₹5,000
  
  Immediately after submitting:
  
  Available Wallet Balance:
  ₹24,923.49
  
  Do NOT reduce the actual wallet balance yet.
  
  Withdrawal request:
  ₹5,000
  Status:
  PENDING
  
  After admin APPROVES:
  
  Wallet balance:
  ₹19,923.49
  
  Withdrawal:
  ₹5,000
  Status:
  APPROVED / PROCESSED
  
  After admin REJECTS:
  
  Wallet balance remains:
  ₹24,923.49
  
  Withdrawal:
  ₹5,000
  Status:
  REJECTED
  
  ==================================================
  4. IMPORTANT — AVOID DOUBLE DEDUCTION
  ==================================================
  
  This is extremely important.
  
  If a withdrawal request is already approved/processed, approving it again must NOT deduct money again.
  
  Implement backend-side protection such as:
  
  - Check current withdrawal status before processing.
  - Only allow wallet deduction when status is PENDING.
  - Use an atomic database update / transaction where appropriate.
  - Store transaction/reference information.
  - Prevent duplicate approval requests.
  - Prevent duplicate wallet ledger entries.
  
  Example:
  
  PENDING → APPROVED = valid
  
  APPROVED → APPROVED = must do nothing / return already processed
  
  REJECTED → APPROVED = must not be allowed unless there is an explicitly designed re-open flow.
  
  ==================================================
  5. WALLET / WITHDRAWAL DATA MODEL
  ==================================================
  
  Inspect the existing models first.
  
  Do NOT blindly create duplicate models.
  
  If the current withdrawal model already exists, extend it.
  
  Withdrawal should support fields similar to:
  
  - userId
  - amount
  - bankAccountId
  - status
  - requestedAt
  - approvedAt
  - rejectedAt
  - processedAt
  - rejectionReason
  - adminId / approvedBy
  - transaction/reference ID
  
  Use the project's existing naming conventions.
  
  Recommended statuses:
  
  PENDING
  APPROVED
  REJECTED
  PROCESSING
  COMPLETED
  
  Only introduce statuses that fit the existing architecture.
  
  ==================================================
  6. USER SUBMIT WITHDRAWAL UI
  ==================================================
  
  When the user clicks:
  
  "Confirm Withdrawal"
  
  DO NOT show a message implying that money has already been transferred.
  
  Instead show:
  
  Title:
  "Withdrawal Request Submitted"
  
  Message:
  
  "Your withdrawal request has been sent to the admin for approval. Once approved, the payment will be credited to your registered bank account."
  
  Example:
  
  Withdrawal Request Submitted
  
  Your withdrawal request has been sent to the admin for approval.
  Once approved, the payment will be credited to your registered bank account.
  
  Button:
  "OK"
  
  Use the app's existing custom modal/toast design instead of a browser/native alert if possible.
  
  ==================================================
  7. USER NOTIFICATION
  ==================================================
  
  Immediately after submitting the withdrawal:
  
  Create/show a notification:
  
  Title:
  "Withdrawal Request Sent"
  
  Message:
  "Your withdrawal request of ₹5,000 has been sent for admin approval. Once approved, the payment will be credited to your registered bank account."
  
  Status:
  PENDING
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT say:
  
  "Payment credited"
  
  "Withdrawal successful"
  
  "Money deducted"
  
  until admin actually approves/processes the withdrawal.
  
  ==================================================
  8. ADMIN APPROVAL NOTIFICATION
  ==================================================
  
  When admin approves the withdrawal:
  
  Create a user notification:
  
  Title:
  "Withdrawal Approved"
  
  Message:
  
  "Your withdrawal request of ₹5,000 has been approved. The payment will be credited to your registered bank account."
  
  If the system has a real payment-processing step, use appropriate wording based on the actual processing state.
  
  Do not claim payment has been credited if the payment has not actually been processed.
  
  ==================================================
  9. ADMIN REJECTION NOTIFICATION
  ==================================================
  
  If admin rejects:
  
  Title:
  "Withdrawal Rejected"
  
  Message:
  
  "Your withdrawal request of ₹5,000 was rejected by admin."
  
  If rejection reason exists, include it:
  
  "Reason: <reason>"
  
  Wallet balance must remain unchanged.
  
  ==================================================
  10. ADMIN WITHDRAWAL MANAGEMENT
  ==================================================
  
  Inspect the existing admin withdrawal section.
  
  Admin should clearly see:
  
  - User name
  - Email
  - Withdrawal amount
  - Bank account
  - Account holder name
  - Requested date/time
  - Current wallet balance
  - Withdrawal status
  - Request ID
  - Previous withdrawal history if available
  
  For PENDING requests, provide:
  
  [Approve] [Reject]
  
  After approval:
  
  Disable the Approve button.
  
  Show:
  
  APPROVED / PROCESSED
  
  For rejected:
  
  Show:
  
  REJECTED
  
  Do not allow accidental duplicate approval.
  
  ==================================================
  11. WALLET BALANCE DISPLAY
  ==================================================
  
  Fix wallet calculations so that:
  
  Available Balance
  = actual wallet balance available to the user
  
  Pending withdrawal
  must NOT permanently reduce the wallet balance before approval.
  
  However, inspect the existing business logic for concurrent withdrawal requests.
  
  If the existing system needs to prevent a user from submitting multiple pending requests exceeding available funds, implement a proper "reserved/pending withdrawal" concept rather than incorrectly deducting the wallet balance.
  
  For example:
  
  Wallet Balance: ₹24,923.49
  Pending Withdrawal: ₹5,000
  Available for New Withdrawal: ₹19,923.49
  
  BUT:
  
  Actual wallet balance should remain ₹24,923.49 until admin approval.
  
  Use the terminology clearly in the UI.
  
  Do not create inconsistent balance calculations.
  
  ==================================================
  12. WALLET UI — PROFESSIONAL FINTECH DESIGN
  ==================================================
  
  Redesign the Wallet screen to look more professional and polished, inspired by modern fintech/trading apps such as Groww.
  
  IMPORTANT:
  Do NOT copy Groww's exact UI, branding, assets, or proprietary design.
  
  Use the screenshots only as inspiration for:
  
  - clean hierarchy
  - spacing
  - typography
  - cards
  - balance presentation
  - transaction sections
  - professional fintech feel
  
  The Wallet screen should contain:
  
  --------------------------------
  Wallet
  Balance & transactions
  --------------------------------
  
  Available Balance
  
  ₹24,923.49
  
  Then a clean summary section:
  
  Total Wallet Balance
  Deposited
  Realized Profit
  Realized Loss
  Current P&L
  Pending Withdrawal
  Charges
  Net Balance
  
  Use clear positive/negative styling.
  
  ==================================================
  13. WALLET ACTION TABS
  ==================================================
  
  Keep the existing wallet actions:
  
  Deposit
  Withdraw
  Bank A/C
  History
  
  Make them look cleaner and more professional.
  
  Active tab should be visually obvious.
  
  Do not break existing functionality.
  
  ==================================================
  14. WITHDRAWAL CARD
  ==================================================
  
  Improve the Withdrawal section.
  
  Professional layout:
  
  Withdrawal Request
  
  Select Bank Account
  
  [ State Bank of India
    **** 7560
    Account holder
    ● ]
  
  Amount
  
  ₹5,000
  
  Available Balance
  ₹24,923.49
  
  Pending Withdrawal
  ₹0.00
  
  Charges
  ₹0.00
  
  Final Amount
  ₹5,000.00
  
  [ Confirm Withdrawal ]
  
  Make the hierarchy clear.
  
  Do not show misleading values.
  
  ==================================================
  15. BANK ACCOUNT
  ==================================================
  
  Keep the existing bank account functionality.
  
  User should be able to:
  
  - Add bank account
  - Select bank account
  - View saved bank accounts
  - Use selected bank account for withdrawal
  
  Do not remove existing bank account functionality.
  
  If bank account verification exists, preserve it.
  
  ==================================================
  16. WITHDRAWAL HISTORY
  ==================================================
  
  The History section should clearly show:
  
  Withdrawal amount
  Date/time
  Bank account
  Status
  
  Example:
  
  ₹5,000
  11 Aug, 3:59 PM
  ****7560
  
  PENDING
  
  or
  
  APPROVED
  
  or
  
  REJECTED
  
  Use clear status badges.
  
  ==================================================
  17. TRANSACTION LEDGER
  ==================================================
  
  Inspect existing wallet transactions.
  
  Make sure the transaction history correctly represents:
  
  - Deposit
  - Withdrawal Request
  - Withdrawal Approved
  - Withdrawal Rejected
  - Profit
  - Loss
  - Charges
  
  Do NOT create a completed withdrawal transaction at the moment of request if the existing accounting system considers the actual debit to occur only after approval.
  
  Avoid duplicate transaction records.
  
  ==================================================
  18. BACKEND + DATABASE CONSISTENCY
  ==================================================
  
  This is NOT just a frontend UI change.
  
  Trace the complete flow:
  
  Mobile App
  ↓
  Withdrawal API
  ↓
  Backend Controller
  ↓
  Withdrawal Service
  ↓
  Wallet Service
  ↓
  Database
  ↓
  Admin Approval
  ↓
  Wallet Ledger
  ↓
  Notification
  ↓
  Mobile App
  
  Find where the current code deducts the wallet balance during request creation.
  
  Move the actual wallet deduction to the APPROVAL transaction.
  
  Do not simply hide the deduction in the UI.
  
  The database/accounting must be correct.
  
  ==================================================
  19. ATOMICITY / RACE CONDITIONS
  ==================================================
  
  Because this is a trading/wallet application, handle concurrency safely.
  
  Admin approval should atomically:
  
  1. Verify withdrawal is PENDING.
  2. Verify wallet balance is sufficient.
  3. Deduct the amount.
  4. Create/update wallet transaction.
  5. Update withdrawal status.
  6. Record admin approval.
  7. Create notification.
  
  If one important step fails, do not leave the wallet and withdrawal status inconsistent.
  
  Use the project's existing MongoDB transaction/session architecture if available.
  
  Do not introduce unnecessary new infrastructure.
  
  ==================================================
  20. DO NOT BREAK EXISTING FUNCTIONALITY
  ==================================================
  
  Everything else must continue working:
  
  - Login
  - Profile
  - Home
  - Portfolio
  - Market
  - Wallet
  - Deposit
  - Bank account
  - Trading
  - Trade assignment
  - Profit
  - Loss
  - Notifications
  - Admin dashboard
  - Admin trading
  - Existing authentication
  - Broker functionality
  
  Only change what is necessary for the withdrawal lifecycle and Wallet UI.
  
  ==================================================
  21. TEST THESE EXACT SCENARIOS
  ==================================================
  
  TEST CASE 1 — PENDING
  
  Wallet = ₹24,923.49
  
  User requests ₹5,000.
  
  Expected:
  
  Wallet actual balance = ₹24,923.49
  Withdrawal = PENDING
  Notification = Withdrawal Request Sent
  
  No permanent wallet deduction.
  
  --------------------------------
  
  TEST CASE 2 — ADMIN APPROVES
  
  Admin approves ₹5,000.
  
  Expected:
  
  Wallet = ₹19,923.49
  Withdrawal = APPROVED/COMPLETED
  Notification = Withdrawal Approved
  
  Only one deduction.
  
  --------------------------------
  
  TEST CASE 3 — ADMIN REJECTS
  
  Wallet = ₹24,923.49
  
  User requests ₹5,000.
  
  Admin rejects.
  
  Expected:
  
  Wallet = ₹24,923.49
  Withdrawal = REJECTED
  Notification = Withdrawal Rejected
  
  No deduction.
  
  --------------------------------
  
  TEST CASE 4 — DOUBLE APPROVAL
  
  Admin attempts to approve the same request twice.
  
  Expected:
  
  Only first approval changes wallet.
  
  Second attempt:
  No additional deduction.
  
  --------------------------------
  
  TEST CASE 5 — INSUFFICIENT BALANCE
  
  User has ₹2,000.
  
  Attempts withdrawal ₹5,000.
  
  Expected:
  Request rejected with a proper message.
  
  Do not allow negative wallet balance.
  
  --------------------------------
  
  TEST CASE 6 — MULTIPLE PENDING REQUESTS
  
  Test multiple pending withdrawal requests.
  
  Ensure the user cannot exploit pending requests to withdraw more than their actual available funds.
  
  Use proper reservation/available-balance logic if required.
  
  ==================================================
  22. FINAL VALIDATION
  ==================================================
  
  After implementation:
  
  - Run backend TypeScript/build.
  - Run mobile app build.
  - Run lint/typecheck if available.
  - Test withdrawal request.
  - Test admin approval.
  - Test admin rejection.
  - Test wallet balance before/after approval.
  - Test notification creation.
  - Test transaction history.
  - Test bank account selection.
  - Test duplicate approval protection.
  - Test insufficient balance.
  - Test multiple pending withdrawals.
  
  At the end provide:
  
  1. Root cause of early wallet deduction
  2. Files changed
  3. Backend changes
  4. Database/model changes
  5. Mobile UI changes
  6. Admin approval changes
  7. Notification changes
  8. Wallet calculation logic
  9. Tests performed
  10. Build result
  
  IMPORTANT FINAL RULES:
  
  - Do NOT add Firebase.
  - Do NOT add mock/fake wallet balances.
  - Do NOT hardcode wallet amounts.
  - Do NOT deduct wallet money on withdrawal request creation.
  - Deduct wallet money ONLY after successful admin approval.
  - Do NOT break existing trading/profit/loss functionality.
  - Do NOT claim payment is credited before actual approval/processing.
  - Keep the implementation production-ready and consistent with the existing architecture.
