  [Clipboard]
  I need you to fix and strictly enforce the withdrawal workflow across my COMPLETE project.
  
  PROJECTS:
  - backend
  - web-frontend
  - mobile-app
  
  I have attached screenshots showing the current behavior.
  
  CURRENT BUG:
  A user submits a ₹1,077 withdrawal request from the mobile app.
  
  The request appears on the Admin Requests page, BUT the user's wallet amount is already being deducted BEFORE the admin approves the request.
  
  THIS IS WRONG.
  
  The wallet amount MUST NOT be deducted when the user only submits a withdrawal request.
  
  ==================================================
  REQUIRED BUSINESS FLOW
  ==================================================
  
  The exact required flow is:
  
  MOBILE APP USER
        ↓
  Select Bank Account
        ↓
  Enter Withdrawal Amount
        ↓
  Submit Withdrawal Request
        ↓
  Backend creates withdrawal with status = PENDING
        ↓
  Admin receives the request
        ↓
  USER WALLET:
        - DO NOT deduct actual wallet balance
        - Show withdrawal as PENDING
        - Show notification that request was sent
        ↓
  ADMIN REVIEWS REQUEST
        ↓
        ┌──────────────────────┐
        │                      │
        ↓                      ↓
     APPROVE                 REJECT
        ↓                      ↓
  Deduct wallet             Do NOT deduct
  amount                    wallet amount
        ↓                      ↓
  Create DEBIT              Release any
  ledger entry              temporary lock
        ↓                      ↓
  Payment becomes           Withdrawal
  successful                becomes REJECTED
        ↓
  User gets notification
        ↓
  Withdrawal history becomes SUCCESS/APPROVED

  [LMB]
  [Clipboard]
  # Portfolio Code Review + Premium Next.js Portfolio Redesign
  
  I am going to provide you with my existing portfolio website repository.
  
  Your task is to **first thoroughly review the existing codebase**, understand what is already implemented, identify problems and improvement opportunities, and then transform it into a **premium, modern, professional, client-facing developer portfolio website using Next.js**.
  
  The final website should be good enough for me to send directly to potential clients, recruiters, and companies.
  
  ---
  
  ## 1. IMPORTANT — DO NOT START CODING IMMEDIATELY
  
  Before making changes, inspect the complete repository.
  
  Analyze:
  
  * Project structure
  * Next.js version
  * React version
  * TypeScript configuration
  * Existing components
  * Pages/routes
  * Layouts
  * Header/navbar
  * Hero section
  * About section
  * Skills section
  * Experience section
  * Projects section
  * Services section
  * Contact section
  * Footer
  * Images/assets
  * Fonts
  * Icons
  * Animations
  * Responsive implementation
  * SEO implementation
  * Metadata
  * Performance
  * Accessibility
  * Reusable components
  * API/backend integrations, if any
  * Environment variables
  * Dependencies
  * Unused code
  * Duplicate code
  * Broken functionality
  * Console errors
  * Build errors
  * Mobile responsiveness
  
  Do not blindly rewrite the project.
  
  First understand what already exists and preserve useful functionality.
  
  ---
  
  # 2. CREATE AN AUDIT REPORT FIRST
  
  Before modifying the code, create:
  
  `PORTFOLIO_CODE_REVIEW.md`
  
  The report should contain:
  
  ### A. Current Architecture
  
  Explain:
  
  * Current framework
  * Folder structure
  * Main pages
  * Important components
  * Data flow
  * Libraries/dependencies
  * Existing UI architecture
  
  ### B. Current Problems
  
  Identify:
  
  * UI/UX problems
  * Poor component structure
  * Code duplication
  * Bad naming
  * Unused dependencies
  * Broken links
  * Broken components
  * Responsiveness issues
  * Performance issues
  * Accessibility issues
  * SEO issues
  * Security issues
  * Poor animations
  * Poor typography
  * Weak visual hierarchy
  
  ### C. What Is Already Good
  
  Clearly mention existing parts that should be preserved.
  
  ### D. Recommended Improvements
  
  Give recommendations grouped into:
  
  1. UI/UX
  2. Design system
  3. Frontend architecture
  4. Performance
  5. SEO
  6. Accessibility
  7. Mobile responsiveness
  8. Animations
  9. Developer experience
  
  ### E. Final Target Design
  
  Describe what the portfolio should look and feel like after the redesign.
  
  Only after this analysis should you start implementing the redesign.
  
  ---
  
  # 3. DESIGN GOAL
  
  Transform the existing portfolio into a:
  
  **Premium + Modern + Minimal + Professional + High-Converting Developer Portfolio**
  
  It should feel like a portfolio created by a highly experienced full-stack engineer, not a basic template.
  
  The design should be:
  
  * Clean
  * Premium
  * Modern
  * Professional
  * Responsive
  * Fast
  * Elegant
  * Interactive
  * Easy to navigate
  * Client-friendly
  
  Avoid:
  
  * Overly flashy animations
  * Excessive gradients
  * Random decorative elements
  * Huge amounts of text
  * Generic template appearance
  * Unnecessary sections
  * Poor contrast
  * Overcrowded layouts
  
  The design should communicate:
  
  **"This developer can build production-grade software."**
  
  ---
  
  # 4. TECHNOLOGY REQUIREMENTS
  
  Use the existing stack where practical.
  
  If the project already uses Next.js, improve it rather than unnecessarily migrating it.
  
  Preferred stack:
  
  * Next.js
  * React
  * TypeScript
  * Tailwind CSS if already available or appropriate
  * Modern reusable components
  * Lucide icons or existing icon library
  * Framer Motion / Motion only where it meaningfully improves UX
  
  Use modern Next.js best practices.
  
  Prefer:
  
  * Server Components where appropriate
  * Client Components only when required
  * Optimized images
  * Proper metadata
  * Semantic HTML
  * Reusable components
  * Clean TypeScript
  * Proper loading/error states where required
  
  ---
  
  # 5. HOMEPAGE STRUCTURE

  ## Navbar
  
  Include:
  
  * Logo/name

  * Skills
  * Experience
  * Projects

  * Sticky
  * Clean
  * Professional
  * Smooth scrolling
  
  Mobile navigation must work perfectly.

  # 6. HERO SECTION
  
  The hero section is extremely important.
  
  It should immediately communicate:
  
  * Who I am
  * What I do
  * My main technology expertise
  * What type of work I can provide

  * MongoDB

  Add a subtle professional visual element, such as:
  
  * Code-inspired UI
  * Developer terminal
  * Architecture visual
  * Technology badges
  * Subtle animated background
  
  Do NOT make the hero look childish or overly animated.

  ---

  Refactor the project where required.
  
  Follow:

  * DRY principles
  * Strong TypeScript typing
  * Reusable components
  * Clear naming
  * Small focused components
  * Centralized data where appropriate

  * Testimonials
  * Job titles
  
  If some information is missing, use the existing repository data or create a clean placeholder that is easy for me to update.
  
  ---
  
  # 22. VISUAL QUALITY STANDARD
  
  Before finishing, compare the final website against the quality level of premium developer portfolios.
  
  The final result should look:
  
  * Premium
  * Modern
  * Professional
  * Client-ready
  * Trustworthy
  * Technically impressive
  
  A client should be able to understand within 10–15 seconds:
  
  1. Who I am
  2. What I specialize in
  3. What technologies I use
  4. What I have built
  5. How they can contact me
  
  ---
  
  # 23. DO NOT BREAK EXISTING FUNCTIONALITY
  
  Before modifying anything:
  
  Understand existing functionality.
  
  After modifications:
  
  Verify that all existing important functionality still works.
  
  If there are APIs or integrations, do not break them just for visual improvements.
  
  ---
  
  # 24. TESTING
  
  After implementation run:
  
  * TypeScript checks
  * ESLint
  * Production build
  * Existing tests if available
  
  Fix all errors and warnings that are caused by your changes.
  
  Also manually inspect:
  
  * Desktop
  * Tablet
  * Mobile
  
  ---
  
  # 25. FINAL REVIEW
  
  After completing the implementation, perform another complete audit.
  
  Check:
  
  ### UI
  
  * Does everything look professional?
  * Is spacing consistent?
  * Is typography good?
  * Are colors consistent?
  * Are animations smooth?
  
  ### UX
  
  * Is navigation intuitive?
  * Are CTAs clear?
  * Can clients quickly understand my profile?
  * Is contact easy?
  
  ### Technical
  
  * Does production build pass?
  * Are there console errors?
  * Is TypeScript clean?
  * Is responsive behavior correct?
  * Is SEO configured?
  
  ### Performance
  
  * Are images optimized?
  * Is unnecessary JS avoided?
  * Are animations performant?
  
  ---
  
  # 26. FINAL REPORT
  
  Create:
  
  `PORTFOLIO_FINAL_REVIEW.md`
  
  Include:
  
  ## 1. Existing Code Review
  
  What you found.
  
  ## 2. Problems Fixed
  
  List all major issues.
  
  ## 3. Design Improvements
  
  Explain the new design.
  
  ## 4. Technical Improvements
  
  Explain architecture/code improvements.
  
  ## 5. SEO Improvements
  
  ## 6. Performance Improvements
  
  ## 7. Responsive Improvements
  
  ## 8. Files Created/Modified
  
  List important files.
  
  ## 9. Testing Results
  
  Mention:
  
  * TypeScript
  * ESLint
  * Build
  * Responsive checks
  
  ## 10. Remaining Recommendations
  
  Mention anything that I should manually add later, such as:
  
  * Real project screenshots
  * Live project URLs
  * Updated social links
  * Custom domain
  * Analytics
  
  ---
  
  # IMPORTANT EXECUTION RULE
  
  Work in this order:
  
  1. Inspect repository
  2. Understand current implementation
  3. Create `PORTFOLIO_CODE_REVIEW.md`
  4. Plan redesign
  5. Implement redesign

  Please update the previous portfolio redesign requirements with the following mandatory changes.
  
  ### 1. ADD ALL MY PROJECTS
  
  Do NOT select only a few projects.

  * Project name
  * Project description
  * My role
  * Technologies used
  * Main features
  * Backend technologies
  * Frontend technologies
  * Database
  * Cloud/DevOps technologies
  * GitHub repository
  * Live demo
  * Project screenshots/images
  * Relevant integrations
  
  Do NOT invent project information.
  
  If project information is available in the codebase but the project is not currently displayed on the website, add it to the portfolio.
  
  ### 2. BETTER PROJECT PRESENTATION
  
  Since I have multiple projects, don't make the page look overcrowded.
  
  Use a professional project system such as:
  
  * Featured Projects
  * All Projects
  * Project categories
  * Technology filters
  * View Details
  * GitHub
  * Live Demo
  
  Possible categories:
  
  * Full Stack
  * Backend
  * Web Applications
  * Mobile Applications
  * Trading / FinTech
  * API / Backend Systems
  * Cloud / DevOps
  * Other
  
  Add a technology/category filter if it improves UX.
  
  The Projects section should remain fast and responsive even if there are many projects.
  
  ### 3. UPDATE MY SKILLS

  ### 7. PORTFOLIO SHOULD SHOW MY FULL TECHNICAL RANGE

  # COMPLETE PREMIUM PORTFOLIO REDESIGN — DO NOT JUST POLISH THE EXISTING UI
  
  I have an existing Next.js developer portfolio.
  
  I have reviewed the current implementation in the browser and attached screenshots of the current website.
  
  The current website is functional, but the UI does NOT look premium/professional enough for sending to clients, recruiters, and companies.
  
  ## MAIN OBJECTIVE
  
  I want you to **completely redesign the visual experience of my existing portfolio** while preserving the useful functionality and real project information.
  
  Do NOT simply change colors, font sizes, or spacing.
  
  I want a **major UI/UX transformation**.
  
  The final result should look like a portfolio made by a professional product designer + senior frontend engineer.
  
  Think:
  
  **Premium SaaS + Senior Developer Portfolio + High-end Agency Website**
  
  The website should immediately communicate:
  
  > "This developer builds serious, production-ready software."
  
  ---
  
  # 1. FIRST — REVIEW THE CURRENT WEBSITE
  
  Before coding, inspect:
  
  * Entire Next.js codebase
  * Existing components
  * Existing sections
  * Existing projects
  * Existing images
  * Existing assets
  * Existing animations
  * Existing responsive implementation
  * Existing navigation
  * Existing contact functionality
  * Existing social links
  * Existing resume functionality
  * Existing skills
  * Existing project data
  
  Also compare the implementation with the attached screenshots.
  
  Create:
  
  `PORTFOLIO_UI_AUDIT.md`
  
  Include:
  
  ### Current Problems
  
  Identify specifically:
  
  * Weak visual hierarchy
  * Excessive empty space
  * Typography problems
  * Repetitive card design
  * Generic sections
  * Project cards looking too similar
  * Weak project storytelling
  * Poor CTA hierarchy
  * Navigation issues
  * Lack of visual identity
  * Lack of premium interactions
  * Mobile UX issues
  * Inconsistent spacing
  * Weak section transitions
  * Weak footer
  * Any unnecessary UI elements
  
  Then create a redesign plan.
  
  ---
  
  # 2. DO NOT KEEP THE CURRENT DESIGN LANGUAGE
  
  The current design uses too many:
  
  * Rounded rectangular cards
  * Similar bordered boxes
  * Purple buttons
  * Generic dark background
  * Repeated card layouts
  * Simple text blocks
  
  This makes the website feel like a template.
  
  Move away from this.
  
  The new design should have a **strong visual identity**.
  
  Use:
  
  * Strong typography
  * Editorial-style layouts
  * Asymmetric sections
  * Large visual elements
  * Better whitespace
  * Strong grid system
  * Subtle borders
  * Layered backgrounds
  * Carefully controlled gradients
  * High-quality project presentation
  * Sophisticated hover states
  * Smooth transitions
  
  Do NOT make every section a card.
  
  ---
  
  # 3. DESIGN DIRECTION
  
  Create a premium dark developer portfolio.
  
  Suggested visual direction:
  
  ### Background
  
  Use a sophisticated dark background, for example:
  
  * Deep black
  * Near-black
  * Dark graphite
  
  But avoid making the entire website look completely flat black.
  
  Use subtle:
  
  * Grid textures
  * Radial gradients
  * Noise texture
  * Glow
  * Depth
  * Section transitions
  
  These must remain subtle.
  
  ---
  
  # 4. TYPOGRAPHY
  
  Typography is extremely important.
  
  Use a modern premium font system.
  
  The typography should have:
  
  * Huge hero headline
  * Strong section headings
  * Clear hierarchy
  * Comfortable body text
  * Proper line-height
  * Good letter spacing
  
  The hero heading should visually dominate the page.
  
  Do not use oversized text everywhere.
  
  Use different scale levels for:
  
  * Hero
  * Section headings
  * Project titles
  * Body
  * Metadata
  * Labels
  
  ---
  
  # 5. NAVBAR REDESIGN
  
  The current navbar is too basic.
  
  Create a premium navigation experience.
  
  Desktop:
  
  * Logo
  * About
  * Projects
  * Services
  * Skills
  * Contact
  * Primary CTA
  
  Keep it clean.
  
  Use a subtle floating/sticky navbar.
  
  Add:
  
  * Glass/blur effect where appropriate
  * Thin border
  * Scroll state
  * Smooth section navigation
  
  Do NOT make it too tall.
  
  Mobile:
  
  Create a proper modern mobile menu.
  
  It must not look like a default hamburger implementation.
  
  ---
  
  # 6. HERO SECTION — MAJOR REDESIGN
  
  The hero should be the strongest section of the entire website.
  
  Do NOT use the current simple:
  
  " I help businesses grow..."
  
  style as-is.
  
  Create a more distinctive senior developer identity.
  
  Example direction:
  
  Small eyebrow:
  
  `FULL-STACK ENGINEER • 3+ YEARS EXPERIENCE`
  
  Main headline:
  
  **I build scalable digital products that turn complex ideas into real software.**
  
  Then a short description explaining:
  
  * Full-stack development
  * Backend engineering
  * APIs
  * Cloud
  * Scalable systems
  * Modern web applications
  
  Use my REAL profile information.
  
  Do not invent experience.
  
  ---
  
  # 7. HERO VISUAL
  
  The current terminal UI is too generic.
  
  Replace it with something much more sophisticated.
  
  Possible concept:
  
  ### Interactive Developer Workspace
  
  Show a beautiful abstract representation of my engineering work.
  
  For example:
  
  * Code/editor visualization
  * Architecture diagram
  * API request flow
  * Backend → database → cloud visualization
  * System architecture
  * Terminal + code
  * Live status indicators
  * Technology nodes
  
  But it must look designed, not like a random fake terminal.
  
  The visual should communicate:
  
  **I build systems, not just websites.**
  
  Add subtle animation.
  
  Do NOT over-animate.
  
  ---
  
  # 8. HERO STATS
  
  Add a small professional stats area.
  
  Only use REAL information.
  
  For example:
  
  `3+ Years Experience`
  
  `20+ Projects`
  
  `Full-Stack Development`
  
  `Available for Freelance`
  
  Only use numbers that can be verified from my actual portfolio/profile.
  
  If exact numbers are unavailable, do not fabricate them.
  
  ---
  
  # 9. ABOUT SECTION
  
  The current About section is too text-heavy and repetitive.
  
  Redesign it completely.
  
  Use an editorial layout.
  
  Example:
  
  LEFT:
  
  `ABOUT ME`
  
  Large statement:
  
  **I turn business requirements into scalable software.**
  
  RIGHT:
  
  Short professional story.
  
  Below:
  
  A compact information row:
  
  * Location
  * Experience
  * Availability
  * Primary specialization
  
  Avoid putting everything inside large bordered cards.
  
  ---
  
  # 10. EXPERIENCE
  
  Create a premium timeline.
  
  Each experience should show:
  
  * Company
  * Role
  * Duration
  * Key responsibilities
  * Technologies
  * Important contributions
  
  Use a timeline/grid layout rather than generic cards.
  
  Focus on:
  
  **What I built and what problems I solved.**
  
  Do not fabricate information.
  
  ---
  
  # 11. PROJECTS — COMPLETELY REDESIGN THIS SECTION
  
  This is extremely important.
  
  The current Projects section looks like repeated dashboard cards.
  
  I DO NOT WANT THAT.
  
  Instead create a **premium case-study style portfolio**.
  
  Each project should feel like a real product case study.
  
  ---
  
  # 12. FEATURED PROJECT
  
  Create one large featured project.
  
  Layout:
  
  LEFT:
  
  * Large project image/mockup
  
  RIGHT:
  
  * Project category
  * Project name
  * Short description
  * Problem
  * Solution
  * Tech stack
  * View project
  * GitHub

  **Trading Platform**
  
  Then explain what was built.

  # 13. ALL PROJECTS
  
  Include **ALL relevant projects from my existing portfolio/repository**.
  
  Do NOT remove projects simply because there are many.
  
  But don't show all projects in identical cards.
  
  Create visual variety.
  
  Possible layouts:
  
  * Featured project
  * Two-column project
  * Full-width project
  * Compact project
  * Case-study style project
  
  Each project should have its own visual identity.

  # 14. PROJECT FILTER
  
  If I have many projects, add:
  
  `All`

  `Frontend`

  `FinTech`
  
  `E-commerce`

  Use the actual project images/assets available in the repository.
  
  Do NOT generate fake project screenshots.
  
  If screenshots are missing:

  Images should have:
  
  * Proper aspect ratio
  * High quality
  * Lazy loading
  * Hover effect
  * Subtle zoom
  * Proper `alt`

  * Testimonials
  * Certifications
  * Performance numbers
  * Project results
  
  Use actual information from my repository and portfolio.
  
  ---
  
  # 32. CODE QUALITY
  
  Keep the implementation clean.
  
  Use:
  
  * Reusable components
  * TypeScript
  * Clean data structures
  * Proper component separation

  Create reusable components for:

  * Hero
  * Section heading

  * Project card
  * Skills
  * Services
  * Contact
  * Footer
  * Buttons
  * Animations

  * WhatsApp
  * LinkedIn
  * GitHub
  * Project URLs
  * Existing APIs
  * Existing functionality
  
  Only improve where required.
  
  ---
  
  # 34. VISUAL QUALITY CHECK
  
  Before considering the work complete, open the website in the browser and visually inspect it.
  
  Do NOT assume it looks good because the code compiles.
  
  Actually check the rendered website.
  
  Ask yourself:
  
  ### Does it look like a premium portfolio?
  
  ### Does it look unique?
  
  ### Does it look like a senior developer built it?
  
  ### Can a client understand my expertise within 10 seconds?
  
  ### Are projects presented like real case studies?
  
  ### Does the page have strong visual hierarchy?
  
  ### Is there too much repetition?
  
  ### Are there unnecessary cards?
  
  ### Is spacing balanced?
  
  ### Does the website feel expensive/premium?
  
  If the answer is NO, continue improving.
  
  ---
  
  # 35. FINAL QUALITY TARGET
  
  The final portfolio should feel closer to:

  * Bootstrap template
  * Generic portfolio template
  * AI-generated landing page
  * Beginner developer portfolio
  * Collection of cards
